the public client is allowed to read `settings/hotelConfig` directly today, and the existing `BookingPage` already does the same fetch; the client-side helper is the smaller blast-radius); add a `paymentMethodLabel` field to the booking doc at create time and read it back on lookup (denormalization doubles the storage and creates a second source of truth that can drift from the admin config — a guest who renames a method weeks later would see the old label on every pre-rename booking; the dynamic read is the canonical "what does the admin say NOW" answer); have the my-booking page import `usePublicSiteContent` to read the bundled `hotelConfig` (the hook fetches the whole `websiteContent` + `roomTypes` + `paymentMethods` + `seniorPwdOnlineEnabled` bundle for the marketing site — pulling it in just to read `paymentMethods[]` is a 10× heavier payload and a 10× heavier read path; a focused `getDoc(settings/hotelConfig)` filtered to `paymentMethods[]` is the right read shape for this surface); rename `LEGACY_PAYMENT_METHOD_LABELS` to `FALLBACK_PAYMENT_METHOD_LABELS` for accuracy (the map is the last-resort fallback, not a "legacy" snapshot — but the existing codebase uses "legacy" for the same semantic; keeping the name consistent with the existing terminology is the lower-friction choice); ship a separate `usePaymentMethodLabel(methodKey)` hook so other future pages can opt in (the helper is one line of `resolvePaymentMethodLabel(methodKey, paymentMethods)` — a hook is overkill for a pure function and would re-introduce the duplication problem if every page owns its own hook instance; the helper is the right granularity; future pages import the same helper + the same `useEffect` shape from `BookingLookupPage` if they need the dynamic fetch); cache the `paymentMethods[]` array across pages via a shared context (broader scope; the cache invalidation story (admin edit + cross-tab) is the public-site cache's responsibility per `usePublicSiteContent`; coupling my-booking to that cache brings the heavyweight bundle back; a per-page focused fetch + the cross-tab invalidation already wired by `usePublicSiteContent` (the `bustPublicSiteContentCache` write) is enough — future per-page migration to a shared cache can be a separate refactor if N pages all need it). **Gates** — #108 / SETTINGS.md §Payment Methods (the contract that `settings/hotelConfig.paymentMethods[]` is the canonical source for guest-facing labels is now enforced on every public surface; no admin-facing surface was touched), MRB-10 (the reservation-scope lookup card's `paymentMethod` render now uses the same helper as the single-booking card; the privacy posture is unchanged — `paymentMethod` was always a wire field, only the displayed label changed), CRL-* (unrelated; the cancellation preview + the destructive cancel + the refund lifecycle are server-side and untouched), the dynamic `paymentMethods` fetch is consistent with the existing `BookingPage.tsx` read of the same doc (same `getDoc` shape, same `typeof method === "string" && typeof label === "string"` filter — the two surfaces are now parallel). **Phase 2 (deferred, NOT in FOL-04)** — extend the shared helper to render a `paymentMethodDescription` (a second field on the admin config) for the receipt PDF + the booking confirmation's expanded details card (the receipt PDF currently shows the same single-line label; a richer multi-line description would be a small admin-config change + a helper extension; out of scope for the FOL-04 label-drift fix); add a cross-page "payment method label" cache that the public-site content cache can invalidate (would avoid the per-page Firestore read when many pages need the same array; the per-page read is cheap today, the cache is only worth it if N > 2 pages grow into the same pattern); add a `paymentMethodIcon` field to the admin config and ship a small icon component so the my-booking card can render the same icon the booking page already shows (UI polish; would unify the visual surface; out of scope for the FOL-04 label-drift fix).

- ✅ **CHD-11.2 — Picker cap raised to soft 10 (no per-type cap on the `+` button) (off-roadmap UX refinement)** (shipped 2026-08-05 on `feature/chd-11-2-picker-cap-soft-10`, per decision #193; v0.260.1 → v0.261.0). Operator-reported 2026-08-05 (post-CHD-11.1 review): "why is it the children + chip is disabled after 3? maybe we can disable that disabling?" The pre-CHD-11.2 surface has a UX issue: the children picker's `+` button is disabled at the room's capacity (e.g., 3 for a Single Room with `maxCapacity: 1`, `maxChildren: 2`, `maxExtraBeds: 1`). The user can pick up to 3 children, but not more — even though the system can validate the over-cap case via the "Fits your group" chip + the submit gate. **Root cause** — the pre-CHD-11.2 `+` button's `disabled` condition was `numChildren >= Math.min(10, selectedMaxSelectableChildren)` (per `BookingPage.tsx:2217`). The `selectedMaxSelectableChildren` is the room's capacity (with auto-bump, per CHD-11.1) — for a Single Room, that's 3 children. The picker is hard-capping at the room's capacity, which is the wrong enforcement surface — per CHD-11's "exploration-first, validation-on-commit" pattern, the picker should be an exploration surface (let the user pick any number), and the "Fits your group" chip + the submit gate are the commit surfaces. **Fix — one-line change to `BookingPage.tsx:2217`.** Change the `+` button's `disabled` condition from `numChildren >= Math.min(10, selectedMaxSelectableChildren)` to `numChildren >= 10` (the CHD-11 soft cap, not the room's capacity). The `selectedMaxSelectableChildren` derivation stays (still used by the "Fits your group" chip + the CHD-11 capacity indicator). The `−` button's disabled condition is unchanged. The `updateChildren` function (with the CHD-11.1 auto-bump) is unchanged. The submit gate is unchanged. **What the user sees after the change** — 1 Single Room, 2 guests, clicks `+` 3 times → 3 children, 3 guests (auto-bump), `+` still enabled. "Fits your group" chip shows. Clicks `+` once more → 4 children, 4 guests (auto-bump), `+` still enabled. "Doesn't fit" chip shows. Clicks `+` repeatedly until 10 children, `+` disabled at the soft cap. The user can now explore freely: pick any number, the chip + submit gate tell them if it works. They can decide whether to add a second room, remove children, or pick a different room type. **Why the soft 10 is the right cap (not the room's capacity, not unlimited)** — the CHD-11 spec's `10` is a UX sanity guard (no one books a 50-child hotel room via the homepage widget). The pre-CHD-11.2 cap of `MIN(10, selectedMaxSelectableChildren)` overrode the 10 with the room's capacity, which is the wrong layer. The new cap `10` is higher than the per-type capacity for most room types (a Family Room's `selectedMaxSelectableChildren` is typically 4-6, so the soft 10 is more permissive), lower than "unlimited" (a sanity guard against pathological inputs), and consistent with the homepage widget's children picker (CHD-13), which also uses 0-10. **What this changes for the data model** — nothing. `numChildren` state stays. `selectedMaxSelectableChildren` derivation stays (used by the chip + capacity indicator). `updateChildren` function is unchanged. `setOccupancy` helper is unchanged. The auto-bump is unchanged. The submit gate is unchanged. **Implementation** — `guest-app/src/pages/BookingPage.tsx` (~5 lines changed: 1 regex update + the comment block). **Tests** — new `guest-app/tests/api/chd-11-2-picker-cap-soft-10.test.ts` (~7 source-text guards: the `+` button's disabled condition is `numChildren >= 10`; the pre-CHD-11.2 condition is gone; the `selectedMaxSelectableChildren` derivation is still present; the `−` button is unchanged; the `updateChildren` auto-bump is unchanged; the `setOccupancy` helper is unchanged; the submit gate is unchanged). **MDs** — spec in `plan/features/BOOKING-FLOW.md §CHD-11.2 — Picker Cap Raised to Soft 10 (No Per-Type Cap on the + Button)`, decision in `plan/docs/DECISIONS-FEATURES.md #193`. **Rejected** — no upper cap at all (a sanity guard is needed; the soft 10 is the right balance); cap at `MIN(20, selectedMaxSelectableChildren)` or some other higher value (the CHD-11 spec's 10 is the right sanity guard); cap at `guests` (the pre-CHD-11.1 behavior; the auto-bump in CHD-11.1 already handles the invariant; the soft 10 is the only cap needed); remove the cap AND remove the auto-bump (violates the CHD-05 contract); show a "Room full" tooltip on the `+` button (a tooltip is a friction point; the "Doesn't fit" chip + the submit gate are the existing surfaces). **Gates** — CHD-05 (server still validates `numAdults + numChildren === numGuests` and `numAdults >= 1`; the client-side picker is just less restrictive; the server is the authoritative gate), CHD-11 (the picker is now a true exploration surface; the "Fits your group" chip + the submit gate are the commit surfaces; the CHD-11 promise is now fully realized), CHD-11.1 (the auto-bump in `updateChildren` is unchanged; the user's experience is smoother because they can now click `+` repeatedly without manually bumping `guests` first), CHD-12 (the per-type "N extra beds" inline pill is unchanged; the data is the same shape; only the picker's cap changes), EXB-11 + EXB-11.1 + EXB-11.2 (the extras toggle + auto-init useEffect are unchanged; the auto-init re-fires when `numAdults` or `numChildren` change), Step 2/3 aside (unchanged), MRB-15-10 + MRB-15-11 (unrelated), CHD-13 (the homepage widget's children picker is also capped at 0-10; the new cap is consistent), EXB-11.3 (no default room-type selection, independent). **Phase 2 (deferred, NOT in CHD-11.2)** — a more aggressive "Fits your group" chip when the user is over the room's capacity; a "Room full" tooltip on the `+` button; a "0 adults + N children" mode for dorm-style rooms; a "Reset to 1 adult + 0 children" quick action; per-room-type picker cap.

- ✅ **CHD-11.1 — Picker auto-bumps guests to fit more children (off-roadmap UX refinement)** (shipped 2026-08-04 on `feature/chd-11-1-picker-auto-bump-guests`, per decision #192; v0.260.0 → v0.260.1). Operator-reported 2026-08-04 (post-EXB-11.3 review): "why is it for the single room, it says up to 1 adult + 2 children but for the picker, the + button is disabled so I am only limited to 1 children." Reproduction: 1 Single Room (`maxCapacity: 1`, `maxChildren: 2`, `maxExtraBeds: 1`), 2 guests (1 adult + 1 child). The user wants to add a 2nd child. The room card's amenity line says "Up to 1 adult + 2 children", but the picker's `+` button is disabled at 1 child. **Root cause** — the pre-CHD-11.1 `updateChildren` function at `BookingPage.tsx:892-903` clamps to `Math.min(Math.max(nextChildren, 0), selectedMaxSelectableChildren, Math.max(0, guests - 1))`. The `Math.max(0, guests - 1)` is the "at least one adult in the booking" invariant (per CHD-05) — a hard cap that prevents the picker from going past `guests - 1` children. With 2 guests, that's 1 child max. The user can't even attempt to add a 2nd child. But the room CAN hold 1 adult + 2 children (= 3 occupants, within the Single Room's soft capacity of `maxCapacity + maxChildren = 1 + 2 = 3` occupants, no extra bed needed). The picker's hard cap is preventing the user from reaching a state the room card says is valid. **Fix — three-part change.** **(1) Refactor `updateGuests` + `updateChildren` to share a `setOccupancy(nextGuests, nextChildren)` helper.** Extract the shared clamping + URL-write logic. Both functions call it. The clamping includes the `safeGuests - 1` floor for children (so `updateGuests(1)` still clamps children to 0). **(2) Change `updateChildren` to auto-bump `guests` when the desired children would leave 0 adults.** Compute `desiredChildren = min(max(nextChildren, 0), selectedMaxSelectableChildren)`, then `newGuests = max(guests, desiredChildren + 1)`, and call `setOccupancy(newGuests, desiredChildren)`. The auto-bump ensures `numAdults = newGuests - desiredChildren >= 1`. **(3) Update `selectedMaxSelectableChildren` derivation to account for the auto-bump.** Loop `N` from 0 to 10 (the soft cap), compute `effectiveGuests = max(guests, N + 1)` and `numAdults = effectiveGuests - N`, check if the overflow fits in `maxExtraBeds`. The highest supported `N` is the cap. The existing `for` loop's bound `Math.max(0, guests - 1)` becomes `10`. **(4) Update the children stepper's `+` button disabled condition** from `numChildren >= Math.min(10, Math.max(0, guests - 1))` to `numChildren >= Math.min(10, selectedMaxSelectableChildren)`. **What the user sees after the change** — 2 guests + 1 child, clicks `+` on children → 3 guests + 2 children (1 adult + 2 children fits the Single Room's soft capacity of 3, no extra bed needed). The `+` button is now enabled at 1 child (was disabled before). Clicks `+` again → 4 guests + 3 children (1 adult + 3 children fits with 1 extra bed). Clicks `+` again → 5 guests + 4 children (doesn't fit, `+` button is now disabled at the room's cap of 3). The CHD-11 capacity chip on the room card updates live (Fits / Tight / Doesn't fit). The "at least 1 adult" invariant is still preserved via auto-bump. **What this changes for the data model** — nothing. `guests` state stays, `numChildren` state stays, `numAdults = max(0, guests - numChildren)` derivation stays. The `setOccupancy` helper is new (extracted from `updateGuests` + `updateChildren`). The `selectedMaxSelectableChildren` derivation is updated (same name, new formula). **Implementation** — `guest-app/src/pages/BookingPage.tsx` (~20 lines changed: extracted helper + refactored functions + updated derivation + updated disabled condition). **Tests** — new `guest-app/tests/api/chd-11-1-picker-auto-bump-guests.test.ts` (~8 source-text guards: the `setOccupancy` helper is defined; `updateGuests` calls it; `updateChildren` does the auto-bump; the `guests - 1` clamp is gone from `updateChildren`; the new derivation uses `effectiveGuests = max(guests, N + 1)`; the `+` button's disabled condition uses `selectedMaxSelectableChildren`). **MDs** — spec in `plan/features/BOOKING-FLOW.md §CHD-11.1 — Picker Auto-Bumps Guests to Fit More Children`, decision in `plan/docs/DECISIONS-FEATURES.md #192`. **Rejected** — don't auto-bump, let user manually bump guests first (2-step action, worse UX); show a hint with a quick action (more UI than auto-bump); remove the "at least 1 adult" invariant entirely (violates CHD-05); keep the existing derivation bounded by `guests - 1` (cap would be wrong for higher children counts); show a modal asking "Bump up to 3 guests?" (modal is friction). **Gates** — CHD-05 (server still validates `numAdults + numChildren === numGuests` and `numAdults >= 1`; the client-side auto-bump maintains the invariant), CHD-11 (the per-type `maxChildren` cap is still the upper bound; the picker just allows the user to explore up to that cap; the capacity chip is the verification surface), EXB-11 + EXB-11.1 + EXB-11.2 (the extras toggle uses the same `requiredExtraBedsFor` helper as the new derivation; the auto-init useEffect re-fires when `numAdults` or `numChildren` change), Step 2/3 aside (extra-bed pricing updates live; no change), MRB-15-10 + MRB-15-11 (admin CRUD + photo gallery, unrelated), CHD-13 (homepage search widget, unrelated; the URL pre-fill is unchanged), EXB-11.3 (no default room-type selection, independent; affects room-type card selection, not children stepper). **Phase 2 (deferred, NOT in CHD-11.1)** — a separate "Adults" stepper on the homepage widget and `/book` picker (instead of the single "Guests" stepper that auto-derives adults); smarter auto-bump with a "room doesn't fit" hint; a "Reset to 1 adult + 0 children" quick action; visual feedback when the auto-bump fires.

### Deferred Loyalty & Audit Tasks
- ⏸ **MED-3 — "Different email" reconciliation (guest self-service half) — Still Deferred.** The guest self-service prompt ("We found bookings under a different email. Would you like to link them?") on the `/rewards` join surface / post-sign-in / post-booking confirmation is the larger of the two MED-3 paths. **Workaround** — guest can still find the booking at `/my-booking` (ref + email); staff can now also link it from the member detail drawer (the front-desk manual link above covers the common case without the guest needing to involve the front desk). `plan/docs/AUDIT-SPARK-REWARDS-REPORT.md §MED-3` and decision #135 + #140.
- ⏸ **LOW-3 — `linkBookingsByEmail` batch not chunked to Firestore's 500-write limit** — **Deferred (theoretical only)**. A single-hotel guest with >500 same-email matches is not realistic at 14-room scale. Revisit if `linkBookingsByEmail` is ever re-pointed at a multi-property or federation-level surface. No code change.

- ✅ **FOL-05 — Verify-payment reservation-scope + dashboard one-card-per-reservation (off-roadmap bug fix)** (shipped 2026-08-07 on `fix/fol-05-verify-reservation-scope`, per decision #201; v0.264.12 → v0.264.13). **REAL BUG FIX** (operator-reported 2026-08-07, post-FOL-04). The operator reported: "for reservations with multiple rooms, can we verify the payment only once? right now, I am seeing 1 row for verification per room". Two related issues from the same root cause. **(1) Dashboard UI** — the pre-FOL-05 list was `bookings.filter(b => b.status === "payment-uploaded")`, a flat per-booking render. A 2-room reservation with both children `payment-uploaded` rendered TWO cards (one per child). The verify amount on each card was the lead room's `totalPrice - onsitePayments.reduce(...)` — not the reservation's. **(2) Server** — the pre-FOL-05 `handleVerifyAndRecordPayment` only flipped the TARGET child's `status` to `payment-confirmed`, even when the cumulative reservation payment covered all siblings (the money was already at the reservation level per MRB-04 Phase 2.x, but the booking-doc status updates were per-room). So even if a guest paid the full reservation in one GCash transfer and the staff verified the full amount on the lead, only the lead flipped to `payment-confirmed` — the second room stayed `payment-uploaded` and the dashboard kept showing the second card. The staff had to verify a second time on the second card (with `min="0.01"` blocking a zero-amount verify). The same shape of bug lived in `handleAddPayment` (the staff-new-payment path) and in `handleRejectPayment` (the rejection path). **Root cause** — money lives at the reservation level post-MRB-04 (the verified payment record lives at `reservations/{id}/payments/{paymentId}`), but the booking-doc `status` transitions were per-room. A single verify call's cumulative payment often covered siblings the handler never touched. The flat per-booking dashboard list amplified the visible bug: the user saw N cards where 1 was the right answer. **Fix — three-part change spanning the dashboard, the server, and the shared mirror contract.** **(1) Dashboard groups children of a `reservationId` into a single `PendingPaymentItem`** — a new `useMemo` at `admin-app/src/pages/DashboardPage.tsx` builds a `Map<reservationId, Booking[]>`, sorts each group by `reservationPosition` (the FOL-05 lead is the first child = the lowest position), and emits one item per group. The item carries reservation-scope `totalPrice` (from the `Reservation` header, falling back to the children-sum for the cold-start race), `paidAmount` (from the `collectionGroup("payments")` aggregate, MRB-12's wire, falling back to the in-memory `onsitePayments` sum), and `dueAmount`. The room breakdown lives in `rooms[]` (per-room `{ bookingId, roomNumber, roomType, totalPrice, status }`). The card renders one row per item with the `R-YYYYMMDD-NNNNN` reservation ref + a `{N} rooms` chip + the reservation-scope total/paid/due. Legacy N=1 paths render as a single-row item keyed by the lead's `bookingId` — byte-equivalent to the pre-FOL-05 surface. **(2) Verify modal pre-fills the amount to the reservation-scope `dueAmount` and shows a per-room coverage preview** — a new `verifyScope` state (`PendingPaymentItem | null`, `null` for the N=1 / legacy case) carries the reservation-scope context. The `openVerifyForm(item)` action sets `verifyTarget` to the lead booking (the verify handler still takes a `bookingId`) + sets `verifyScope` to the item + pre-fills `verifyAmount` to `item.dueAmount`. The modal renders a "Coverage preview" panel listing each room with a "Cleared" / `{N} still owed` / "Pending" badge that updates live as the staff edits the amount. The preview is a CLIENT-SIDE approximation; the server is the source of truth (a fresh `runTransaction` reads the live state on submit). **(3) Server's three payment handlers gain the sibling-flip pass** — `(a)` pre-read children for the reservation BEFORE any writes (`adminDb.collection("bookings").where("reservationId", "==", bookingReservationId)`, hoisted into the read phase per FOL-03); `(b)` compute `postUpdateChildStatuses` — for verify / add-payment, the rule is "if the child's current status is `pending` or `payment-uploaded` AND the new cumulative reservation payments cover its `totalPrice`, the post-update status is `payment-confirmed`"; for reject, the rule is "every child currently in `payment-uploaded` flips to `pending`"; `(c)` queue per-sibling `transaction.update` calls — each flipped sibling gets its own `transaction.update(bookings/{id}, { status: "payment-confirmed" / "pending", paymentConfirmedAt: now / paymentRejectedAt: now, handledBy: staffUid, holdExpiresAt: ..., updatedAt: now })`; `(d)` update the reservation header's `paymentStatus` via the N>1 aggregate — `paymentStatus: computeReservationAggregatePaymentStatus(postUpdateChildStatuses)`, not the pre-FOL-05 single-child mapper. The pre-FOL-05 `if (fullyPaid && bookingReservationId.length > 0)` and `if (transitionedToPaymentConfirmed && bookingReservationId.length > 0)` guards on the header mirror are REMOVED — the FOL-05 mirror fires on EVERY verify / add for new reservations (a partial that flips zero siblings still leaves the aggregate unchanged, and a partial that flips N siblings correctly surfaces the new aggregate). The new guard is `if (bookingReservationId.length > 0 && siblingChildBookings.length > 0)`. The status check is RESERVATION-AWARE — the pre-FOL-05 verify handler threw `ALREADY_CONFIRMED` whenever the target was `payment-confirmed` or `confirmed`; the FOL-05 check RELAXES this for N>1: a target already past the money gate + flippable siblings proceeds (the target's own status update is skipped because its post-update status equals its pre-update status), but the sibling-flip pass still runs. N=1 + no flippable siblings throws `ALREADY_CONFIRMED` (preserves the pre-FOL-05 contract for the legacy surface). The same relaxation applies to `handleRejectPayment`. The `siblingFlippedCount` / `siblingRejectedCount` field on the 200 OK response surfaces the flip count to the admin UI (the post-verify success modal renders a "X more rooms cleared — {reservationRef}" breadcrumb when the count is > 0; the bell panel emits a separate `payment` notification with the same breadcrumb). **What the user sees after the change** — the dashboard's pending-payments list shows one card per reservation (a 2-room reservation renders 1 card, not 2). The verify modal pre-fills the amount to the reservation's outstanding balance (e.g. ₱10,000 for a 2-room reservation), and the per-room coverage preview shows "Room 101 → Cleared" / "Room 102 → Cleared" before the staff hits submit. The server flips BOTH rooms in one transaction. The card drops off the dashboard on the next snapshot. For a partial payment (e.g. ₱5,000 of ₱10,000), the preview shows "Room 101 → Cleared" / "Room 102 → ₱5,000 still owed", the server flips the one covered child, the second room stays `payment-uploaded`, and the card stays on the dashboard with the remaining balance. The reject path is symmetric: a reservation-scope reject flips every `payment-uploaded` sibling back to `pending` in one transaction. **What this changes for the data model** — nothing. The write paths write to the same fields; the new sibling-flip pass adds per-sibling `transaction.update` calls. The reservation header's `paymentStatus` is already a N>1 aggregate field per MRB-05 / MRB-12 — the FOL-05 change just routes the verify / add / reject mirrors through the aggregate helper instead of the single-child mapper. **What this changes for the email + notification side** — the post-verify success modal surfaces a "X more rooms cleared" breadcrumb (the PRC-13 email + the bell `payment` notification are unchanged — the original `payment-confirmed` email already covered the money event when the FIRST room flipped; the sibling flips are metadata for the staff, not a new guest-facing event). The post-reject path adds a symmetric breadcrumb. **What this changes for the dashboard** — the StatsCard's "Pending Payments" count is now the count of grouped items (which equals the count of proofs queued — the new contracts the proof = the reservation's proof, not the per-room proof). The recent-bookings strip is unchanged (per-booking shape, byte-equivalent). **What this changes for the BookingsPage** — nothing. The BookingsPage already groups multi-room reservations into one row (per MRB-07 / MRB-12) and the per-child verify reachable from the drawer's Folio section uses the same handler, which now correctly flips all covered siblings when verified against a single child. The table view's behaviour is unchanged. **Implementation** — 1 new test file (fol-05-verify-reservation-scope.test.ts, 23 guards) + 1 new test file (fol-05-dashboard-pending-payments.test.ts, 15 guards) + 3 modified test files (the MRB-04 Phase 3 source-text pin) + 4 modified source files (guest-app/server/handlers/bookings.ts — the 3 handler bodies + the post-transaction side effects; admin-app/src/context/AdminContext.tsx — the `verifyAndRecordPayment` + `rejectPayment` return types + inline Promise annotations; admin-app/src/pages/DashboardPage.tsx — the grouped `useMemo` + the card render + the verify modal with the per-room coverage preview + the verify success modal's reservation-scope math; the `dashboard-payment-actions.test.ts` — the `title` attribute is now a dynamic variable, the test matches the new shape). ~200 lines of new code in `bookings.ts` (the sibling pre-read + the `postUpdateChildStatuses` computation + the per-sibling `transaction.update` writes + the aggregate mirror, applied 3 times) + ~150 lines of new code in `DashboardPage.tsx` (the grouped `useMemo` + the reservation-aware card render + the per-room coverage preview). **Tests** — 23 source-text guards in `guest-app/tests/api/fol-05-verify-reservation-scope.test.ts` (per-handler slice boundaries, the FOL-03 reads-before-writes invariant on all 3 handlers, the `postUpdateChildStatuses` array name + the flip rule, the per-sibling `transaction.update` writes, the aggregate mirror write, the reservation-aware status check, the response field surfacing, the shared FOL-05 contract across all 3 handlers) + 15 source-text guards in `admin-app/src/__tests__/fol-05-dashboard-pending-payments.test.ts` (the `PendingPaymentItem` type at module scope, the grouped `useMemo` computation, the per-reservationId grouping key, the lead-by-position sort, the `collectionGroup("payments")` aggregate read, the dashboard card render with the `{N} rooms` chip + reservation-scope labels, the `openVerifyForm(item)` call site, the `verifyScope` state, the per-room coverage preview, the verify modal's reservation-scope math for the success modal, the rejection modal's reservation-scope wording) + the 3 pre-existing MRB-04 Phase 3 source-text tests (`mrb-04-phase-2x-refund-verify.test.ts` + `mrb-04-phase-3-reservation-payment-status.test.ts` + `mrb-15-08-legacy-fallback.test.ts`) are updated to match the FOL-05 contract (the helper is now `computeReservationAggregatePaymentStatus(postUpdateChildStatuses)`, the gate is now `bookingReservationId.length > 0 && siblingChildBookings.length > 0`, the pre-FOL-05 `fullyPaid` / `transitionedToPaymentConfirmed` guards are GONE). All 9 pre-existing test failures (4 in `bookings-staff-email-dedup.test.ts` + the firebase emulator tests + 1 in `convert-inquiry-read-order.test.ts` which is flaky) are unrelated to FOL-05, also failing on the dev baseline. **MDs** — spec in `plan/features/BOOKINGS-MANAGEMENT.md §FOL-05` (this entry), decision in `plan/docs/DECISIONS-FEATURES.md #201`, pattern documented in `plan/docs/GOTCHAS.md` as an extension of the FOL-03 "Firestore `runTransaction` requires all reads before all writes" gotcha + the MRB-04 "reservation-owned payment subcollection" pattern. **Rejected** — keep the per-room sub-step modal in the verify flow (forces N staff clicks for the same end state; one GCash / bank transfer covers the whole reservation, so the per-room sub-steps add friction without value; the user's explicit "go with option A" preference); add a new `verify-reservation` endpoint that takes a `reservationId` and flips all children (would duplicate the FOL-05 sibling-flip logic on the server side without enabling a different UX; the FOL-05 verify flow is "use the existing `handleVerifyAndRecordPayment` endpoint with the lead booking + the server flips all covered siblings" — the same endpoint, the same contract, the same response shape, just enriched with the `siblingFlippedCount` field); compute the per-child flip client-side and send N verify requests (loses atomicity — a staff-verifies-2-rooms-then-network-blips race could leave one room in `payment-uploaded` and one in `payment-confirmed`; the FOL-05 single-transaction approach is atomic); add a denormalized `paidAmount` field on the reservation header that the dashboard reads instead of the `collectionGroup("payments")` aggregate (denormalization creates a second source of truth that can drift; the aggregate is the canonical "sum every payment" answer; a future "we'll also denormalize the per-room paid amount" pattern doubles the storage + the bug surface; the FOL-05 dashboard uses the existing aggregate wire the BookingsPage reservation row already uses); apply FOL-05 only to the verify path and leave the reject path at the per-room scope (asymmetric UX — the staff would have to click reject once per room; the same FOL-05 pattern closes the same shape of bug for all 3 payment handlers; the cost is ~50 lines per handler for the symmetric behavior); use a Firestore `writeBatch` for the per-sibling updates (batches don't support transactional reads; the FOL-05 mirror needs to be atomic with the sibling flips — a batched write could leave the header stale if a concurrent verify fired between the batch and the mirror read; the `runTransaction` + per-sibling `update` pattern is the correct shape); expose the per-room coverage preview as a separate "preview" endpoint (the preview is a CLIENT-SIDE approximation from the live data the dashboard already has in memory — a server endpoint would add latency for a UX hint that doesn't need to be authoritative; the server is the source of truth on submit, the preview is just a hint). **Gates** — MRB-04 (the canonical vs legacy subcollection split is preserved — the FOL-05 verify / add-payment / reject handlers still read the payments subcollection from the reservation (new reservations) or the booking (legacy); the sibling-flip pass writes per-sibling status fields on the booking doc, which is the same path the pre-FOL-05 single-child update used), MRB-05 (the N>1 aggregate helper is the canonical reservation-scope payment-status reader; FOL-05 routes all 3 mirror writes through the aggregate instead of the single-child mapper, which is the N>1-correct shape), MRB-12 (the admin reservation-scope paid-amount aggregate is the dashboard's data source — same wire the BookingsPage reservation row already reads; the FOL-05 dashboard's `pendingPaymentItems` computation reads the same `reservations` + `reservationPaidAmount` slice MRB-12 added), MRB-13 (the cancellation-scope sibling-flip pattern is the same shape — both batch operations on multiple children of a reservation; MRB-13 is the cancel-scope precedent FOL-05 builds on), MRB-15-03 (the sibling-flip pass is inside the same `runTransaction` as the target update + the reservation header mirror — atomic across all 3 writes; no partial states where the target flipped but the siblings didn't, or the siblings flipped but the header didn't update), FOL-03 (the sibling pre-read sits BEFORE the first write per the FOL-03 "reads before writes" rule; the per-sibling `transaction.update` calls are placed after all reads; the pre-FOL-05 check-in / check-out ordering fix is the template the FOL-05 sibling-flip pass follows), FOL-01 (the `isPaymentVerified(booking)` helper is unchanged — the FOL-05 sibling flips correctly set the `paymentConfirmedAt` timestamp on every covered child, so the helper returns `true` for all of them; the post-FOL-05 UI's "Verified" badge lights up correctly for every covered child), FOL-02 (the admin mapper's MRB-01 cluster hydration is unchanged — the dashboard's grouped `useMemo` reads the same `reservationId` / `reservationPosition` fields the FOL-02 mapper hydrates), PRC-13 (the verify modal's pre-fill math uses the reservation-scope `dueAmount` instead of the per-room `totalPrice - onsitePayments.reduce(...)`; the success modal's `isFullPayment` + `remainingBalance` now reflect the reservation-scope math via `verifyScope.paidAmount`; the FOL-05 contract is "verify the whole reservation in one click, with the per-room coverage preview in the modal"). **Phase 2 (deferred, NOT in FOL-05)** — apply the same FOL-05 sibling-flip pattern to `handleApplyDiscount` (the discount path) so a reservation-scope discount covers N children in one click (parallel scope; the MRB-12 discount modal already has a "This room" / "All N rooms" segmented control that calls the existing per-child apply-discount endpoint N times — the FOL-05 pattern would consolidate that into a single transactional sibling-flip pass; out of scope for the verify-payment bug); expose the per-room coverage preview server-side (a `GET /api/reservations/{id}/coverage-preview?amount=X` endpoint that returns the per-room "Cleared" / `{N} still owed" / "Pending" badges — would be authoritative but adds latency for a UX hint; the client-side approximation is the smaller blast-radius); add a runtime test that exercises the verify happy path against the Firestore emulator for a multi-room reservation (the existing `bookings-payments-multi-room.test.ts` would be the right home; the source-text guards are the cheap, deterministic pin; the runtime round-trip is the local-env test pattern from MRB-11 / CRL-09 / MRB-14, deferred to the same environment); apply FOL-05 to the corporate-inquiry → reservation conversion path (the convert-inquiry handler creates N children; a parallel FOL-05 sibling-flip pass would consolidate the N `transaction.create` calls — out of scope for the verify-payment bug); add a `paymentStatus` per-room preview on the BookingsPage reservation row's tooltip (the BookingsPage already surfaces the reservation-scope `paymentStatus` via the MRB-12-02 pill; a per-room tooltip would be a deeper drilldown — future UI refinement). **Broader refactor — see [BAR-01 Denormalize rooms into the booking doc (BOOKING-UNIFIED)](#booking-architecture-refactor-bar--deferred-target-post-mrb-15) (deferred, post-MRB-15).** The FOL-05 sibling-flip pass is the right patch for the current bug, but the broader shape (1 booking, 1 lifecycle, 1 payment path, N rooms in an array) is the destination. The FOL-05 sibling-flip pass + the FOL-03 reads-before-writes ordering + the MRB-15-03 counter-proliferation all go away in step 6 of the BOOKING-UNIFIED migration. The 23 FOL-05 server source-text guards + the 15 FOL-05 admin source-text guards are deleted in that step.

- ✅ **Concurrent front-desk call claim is an atomic `runTransaction` (off-roadmap operator question, 2026-08-19)** (shipped 2026-08-19 on `feature/call-staff-claim-transaction`, per decision #214; v0.273.4 → v0.275.0). Operator question 2026-08-19: "for the voice call feature, when a guest calls from the intercomm, what happens if multiple frontdesks are logged in at the same time?" The pre-#214 surface was a best-effort last-write-wins `updateDoc` race that let two front-desk staff both build a peer connection to the same guest — the losing staff ended up with a non-functional "Connected" banner (no working audio) and there was no record of which staff actually took the call. **Fix — four-part change spanning `AdminContext.acceptCall`, the snapshot mapper, the inbox banner, and the call-history system message.** **(1) `runTransaction` claim in `acceptCall`** — the first step of `acceptCall` is now `runTransaction(db, async (tx) => { … })` that reads `calls/{roomId}`, checks `data.status === "ringing"`, and writes `status: "active" + endedAt: null + acceptedBy: { uid, name, claimedAt: serverTimestamp() }` in one commit. The first staff to commit wins; every subsequent staff aborts and surfaces a friendly "Call taken" toast. **(2) `acceptedBy` field on `calls/{roomId}`** — the transaction stamps `{ uid, name, claimedAt }` so the snapshot listener can hydrate it for every tab. Pre-#214 docs map to `null` so the legacy "Connected" UI keeps working on legacy data. **(3) `IntercomInboxPage` three-state banner** — the banner branches on `incomingCall.status === "active" && incomingCall.acceptedBy?.uid !== currentUser?.uid` (the new `isClaimedByOtherStaff` flag). When true, it renders an informational grey surface ("Call Already Answered — Answered by {Name}. This banner clears when they hang up.") with NO Accept/Ignore/Mute/Disconnect buttons. The `data-testid="call-already-claimed-banner"` testid pins this surface. **(4) Staff attribution in the call-answered audit trail** — `recordCallHistory` writes a structured `callAnsweredByName: string | null` field on the `intercoms/{roomNumber}/messages` system doc and prefixes the human-readable `text` with "Call answered by {Name} at {clock} · {duration}" when `adminCallLocalAcceptRef.current === true`. **What the user sees after the change** — two staff clicking Accept within ~1s of each other: exactly one audio connection is established; the losing staff sees a grey "Call Already Answered by Maria" banner; the chat thread records "Call answered by Maria at 2:14 PM · 3m 22s" with the winner's display name. **Known gap filed for next audit** — the `IntercomMessage` snapshot mapper has been silently dropping `messageType`, `callStartedAt`, and `callDuration` since the call-history-messages feature shipped (FOL-02 pattern); #214 added `callAnsweredByName` to the mapper but the pre-existing drops are a separate audit ticket. **Implementation** — 1 new test file (`call-staff-claim-transaction.test.ts`, 23 source-text tests across 7 describe blocks) + 4 modified source files (`AdminContext.tsx` for the `IncomingCall.acceptedBy` type + the snapshot mapper hydration + the `acceptCall` `runTransaction` refactor + the `recordCallHistory` staff attribution; `IntercomInboxPage.tsx` for the three-state banner + the `currentUser` import from `useAdmin`; `shared/types/index.ts` for the canonical `IntercomMessage.callAnsweredByName?: string | null`; `shared/VERSION.ts` for the MINOR bump). ~200 lines of new code in `AdminContext.tsx` + ~50 lines in `IntercomInboxPage.tsx`. **Tests** — 23 source-text guards in `admin-app/src/__tests__/call-staff-claim-transaction.test.ts` (the `runTransaction` shape with `tx.get(callRef)`, the `data.status !== "ringing"` abort, the `acceptedBy: { uid, name, claimedAt: serverTimestamp() }` write, the `notify.info` catch + the return, the post-claim `endedReason: "accept-failed"` write, the snapshot mapper `acceptedBy` hydration, the `IncomingCall` + `CallAcceptedBy` type declarations, the `adminCallLocalAcceptRef` lifecycle, the `recordCallHistory` `callAnsweredByName` write + the text prefix, the `IntercomInboxPage` `isClaimedByOtherStaff` branch + the `call-already-claimed-banner` testid + the no-buttons-in-claim-path check, the `shared/types/index.ts` canonical type declaration). All other admin + shared tests pass (the 19 pre-existing failures in worktree / emulator / guest-app tests are unrelated, also failing on the dev baseline). **MDs** — spec at `plan/features/INTERCOM-INBOX.md §Incoming call notification` (the three-state banner + the `call-already-claimed-banner` testid), `plan/features/INTERCOM-GUEST.md §Voice Call` (the staff-side claim is a staff concern; the guest's peer connection is unchanged), `plan/docs/BACKEND.md` (new `calls/{roomId}` schema section with the `acceptedBy` field, the `endedReason` audit stamps, and the rule-layer trust gap), `plan/docs/TYPES.md §WebRTC Voice Call` (the new `CallAcceptedBy` sub-interface + the `WebRTCCall.acceptedBy?` optional field + the `endedReason?` audit enum), `plan/docs/SECURITY.md §calls` (the deferred hardening to `isStaff()` writes), decision #214 in `plan/docs/DECISIONS-FEATURES.md`. **Gates** — INTERCOM-INBOX (three-state banner is a strict superset of the pre-#214 surface; legacy call docs continue to render the Connected UI byte-equivalent), INTERCOM-GUEST (guest flow is unchanged), INTERCOM-AUDIO-ROUTING (the WebRTC remote stream + ringtone + chime work for the staff that won the claim; the loser tab never builds a peer connection so its `useAudioRouting` never attaches a remote stream), FOL-03 (transaction body reads BEFORE writes; the helper is the gate that aborts the rest of the flow), FOL-02 (snapshot mapper hydration extends to the new `callAnsweredByName` field; the pre-existing `messageType` / `callStartedAt` / `callDuration` drops are a separate audit ticket). **Rejected** — keep the pre-#214 last-write-wins `updateDoc` (the race leaves the wrong staff with a dead peer connection and no audit trail), use a Firestore security rule to gate the accept (the rule layer can't enforce "first wins" atomically; the SDK's `request.auth.uid` check would be a tie, not a claim), move the call claim to a Cloud Function (Vercel Hobby is at the function cap; the claim is a single Firestore read + a single write; doing it in the client SDK avoids the round-trip and the function-count tax), add a "I'll take it" round-robin assignment (overkill for a 3-5-person desk; the claim is "first-click-wins" which is the simplest fair shape), use a separate `callClaims/{roomId}` collection with a `claimedBy` doc (the claim belongs to the same call doc; a separate collection would require a cross-collection transaction; the call doc is the single source of truth), show the acceptedBy name to the guest (the guest's UI doesn't need to know which staff took the call; the staff attribution is an internal audit trail, not a guest-facing concern). **Phase 2 (deferred, NOT in #214)** — narrow the `calls` collection Firestore rule to `isStaff()` writes after the consent + per-room occupancy gate lands; hydrate the pre-existing `messageType` / `callStartedAt` / `callDuration` drops in the `IntercomMessage` snapshot mapper so the chat panel can render the centered "Call answered" footer row with structured data; per-call round-robin or "queued claim" UX if the operator reports the race-condition feel on a real desk.

- ✅ **FOL-02 call-history-messages mapper closure (off-roadmap contract-pin fix, 2026-08-19)** (shipped 2026-08-19 on `fix/fol-02-call-history-mapper-drops`, per decision #215; v0.274.0 → v0.274.1). The #214 PR shipped the `IntercomMessage` mapper hydration for `messageType` / `callStartedAt` / `callDuration` (the three pre-existing drops) alongside the new `callAnsweredByName` field — but only `callAnsweredByName` had a test pin, and the mapper comment still claimed the three fields were "pre-existing drops ... a separate audit ticket" when they were already hydrated. **Fix — three-part change.** **(1) Mapper comment at `AdminContext.tsx:3371-3382` rewritten** to point at the new test file + drop the misleading "pre-existing drops" claim + correct the decision reference from #206 to #214 (the file already had #206..213 by the time #214 shipped). **(2) New test file `admin-app/src/__tests__/fol-02-call-history-mapper-drops.test.ts`** with 6 source-text tests in 2 describe blocks: 4 per-field hydration pins (one test per call-history field, each pinning the exact normalization shape — the `callStartedAt` Timestamp→ISO conversion, the `callDuration` `typeof === "number"` defensive coercion, the `messageType` `|| undefined` shape, the `callAnsweredByName` `typeof === "string"` defensive coercion with `null` for the absent case) + 2 contract-coverage pins that enumerate the always-on + optional fields the mapper already hydrates. The mapper-body slice uses `indexOf` + brace-counting for the closing `});` so the slice is robust to nested function bodies (FOL-02 / MRB-15-10 hydrate-mapper pattern: the slice must end at the mapper's true close, not at a nested `}` substring). **(3) Decision #215 in `DECISIONS-FEATURES.md`** records the closure + the misleading-comment fix + the per-field test pattern. **What the user sees after the change** — nothing different (the mapper was already hydrating the 4 fields after #214; #215 pins the contract). **What this changes for the data model** — nothing. **What this changes for the test surface** — 6 new source-text tests in a dedicated file (separate from `call-staff-claim-transaction.test.ts` because the FOL-02 contract-enforcement test is scoped to the mapper, not to the runTransaction claim — different concern, different test file, per the project's "one test file per concern" convention). All 23 prior #214 tests still pass. **Known follow-up not in #215** — the `IntercomChatPanel` renderer at `IntercomChatPanel.tsx:182-204` does NOT currently read `callAnsweredByName` (the staff name) or `callDuration` (the seconds) — the text body already carries the formatted "Call answered by {Name} at {clock} · {duration}" line written by `recordCallHistory`, so the structured data is available for any future UI polish that wants to render a dedicated "Answered by Maria · 3m 22s" pill instead of relying on the text body. Deferred — the current text-body render is honest + readable, and the structured-data upgrade is a UI refinement, not a bug fix. **MDs** — `plan/docs/DECISIONS-FEATURES.md #215` (this entry), `plan/docs/GOTCHAS.md` is unchanged (the FOL-02 pattern entry is the contract; #215 is a follow-up closure, not a new pattern), `plan/features/INTERCOM-INBOX.md` is unchanged (the FOL-02 closure is read-side, the feature spec's Manual QA already covers the renderer behavior). **Rejected** — fold the #215 fix into the #214 PR as an amendment (the #214 PR is already merged to dev; amending a merged PR adds friction without value; a focused separate commit is the cleaner audit trail), pin only the 3 pre-existing drops without `callAnsweredByName` (the FOL-02 pattern requires a per-field test for EVERY contract field the mapper hydrates; the 4th field's absence from the test file is a future audit gap), add a behavioural test that exercises the full chat-panel render against a fake IntercomMessage (a behavioural test would need a React Testing Library setup + a stub for the `useAdmin` context + a renderer mock — the source-text test is the cheap, deterministic FOL-02-style pin the project uses for mapper closures), use a more general "enumerates every IntercomMessage field" test instead of per-field tests (the per-field test pattern is the FOL-02 contract enforcement; a single "all fields present" test would miss a future refactor that drops one field while preserving the others — the per-field pattern is the bug-finding shape, not the coverage shape), bump the version MAJOR (the change is a test-pinning + comment fix on top of an already-shipped mapper; no API contract change, no new field, no new wire shape; `fix:` per Conventional Commits triggers a PATCH bump per `Husky` auto-versioning).

### Open Operator-Reported Bugs — Verification 2026-08-19 (REVISED + LIVE FIXES TRACKED)
> Source: the operator's Spark-Inn-Manual-Test-Cases bug dump (Guest App Tests, Admin App Tests, Other Bugs Found tabs), 2026-08-19. The agent + 3 leaf subagents (delegation `deleg_894e86c0`) walked each item against current `dev` HEAD source. **Of the 20 actionable items, 8 are already fixed, 1 is environmental/config, 1 is admin-UI-only (role gate), and 10 are still open real bugs.** Subagent cross-checking surfaced 4 corrections to the agent's first-pass verdicts: BK-05 (cancel reason), #11 (confirmation email), #18 (wordmark overlap), Q-03/#22 (detached `<a>` download) — see the **🔁 Revision log** at the bottom of this section for the row-level deltas. All findings carry the file:line ref + the verbatim code excerpt that proves the verdict.

#### ✅ Live fix shipped (2026-08-19, decisions #220)

- **#20 + #21 + RM-05** — Members page token refresh + Delete Room admin gate (shipped 2026-08-19 on `fix/admin-listener-and-permission-gates` @ `ff832db`, merged via `chore(merge)` @ `e4fa1bd`, v0.274.6 → v0.274.7). The Members listener at `admin-app/src/context/AdminContext.tsx:2985-3019` now mirrors the reservations listener shape (line 1820-1832) — `void auth.currentUser?.getIdToken(true).then(() => { if (cancelled) return; unsubscribe = onSnapshot(...) })` with `cancelled` race guard for the unmount-mid-refresh case + `.catch` for refresh-token failures. The Delete Room button in `RoomsPage.tsx` is now disabled for non-admin staff with an "Only administrators can delete rooms." tooltip; `deleteRoom` in `AdminContext.tsx:1282` short-circuits BEFORE the room-existence check (so no partial storage cleanup / Firestore writes) when `currentUser?.role !== "admin"`. Three-layer regressions: 1 new test file (`rooms-delete-admin-only.test.ts`, 7 source-text guards) + updated `admin-members-listener.test.ts` to pin the new cleanup-arrow shape + the MRB-15-09 wrapper presence + updated `phase-11.8-room-crud.test.ts` to pin the role gate + tooltip. 1438/1438 admin-app tests pass; tsc clean.

- **BK-05** — Booking cancellation requires a reason at UI + server (shipped 2026-08-19 on `fix/bookings-cancel-reason-required`, decision #221). UCO/CLS-01 covered the checkout-with-balance path (`ConfirmStatusModal` at `BookingsPage.tsx:2471`) but did NOT touch the three booking-cancel `ConfirmForm` surfaces — the cancel-reason field carried the `(optional)` label with `reasonRequired={false}` default. The fix has three parts: (1) `BookingsPage.tsx:6447` (booking-cancel modal) + `:6719` + `:6754` (the two store-order cancel modals) now pass `reasonRequired={true}` and flip the label to `"Cancellation reason (required)"`. (2) The server `handleCancelBooking` at `guest-app/server/handlers/bookings.ts:5984` short-circuits with `return res.status(400).json({ success: false, error: "CANCELLATION_REASON_REQUIRED", ... })` when `isStaffCancellation && !validReason.trim()` — mirrors the existing UCO `UNPAID_REASON_REQUIRED` pattern at line 9643. (3) Guest cancellations are NOT gated here (they go through `guestCancelSchema` in `apiRouter.ts` with their own min-length validation). Regression: 1 new test file `admin-app/src/__tests__/bookings-cancel-reason-required.test.ts` (6 source-text guards: booking-cancel UI gate, 2 store-order UI gates, no `(optional)` label anywhere, `ConfirmForm` contract unchanged, server-side 400 with `CANCELLATION_REASON_REQUIRED`, server only gates staff cancellations). 1444/1444 admin-app tests pass (was 1438 before; +6 net). Negative-test confirms gate removal breaks 2 server-side tests with labeled messages.

- **#1 (hero images)** — Static-fallback URLs missing the Google `=wN` size token (shipped 2026-08-19 on `fix/hero-images-size-token`, decision #222, XS scope). The `buildHeroSrcSet` helper in `guest-app/src/components/HeroImage.tsx:65` bails out (`/[sw]\d+$/` regex) when the URL base doesn't end with a recognised size token — so `homepageHeroImage` + `rewardsHeroImage` (both `lh3.googleusercontent.com/aida-public/...` AI-generated content URLs) never got a responsive `srcset` and the browser downloaded the full-size original on every viewport. The append `=w1920` token to both URLs makes them eligible for the `640 / 1080 / 1920` width set the helper emits; the Unsplash URLs (about + corporate) already shipped with `w=1600` and were unaffected. Regression: 1 new test file `guest-app/src/__tests__/hero-image-size-token.test.ts` with 4 source-text guards (homepageHeroImage ends with `=w\d+`, rewardsHeroImage ends with `=w\d+`, both Unsplash URLs still carry `w=` params, HeroImage.tsx still has the `=[sw]\d+$` bail-out regex). tsc clean; guest-app tests 4/4 net pass.

| **#20 + #21** — Members page blank white screen (desktop + mobile) | AdminApp/MembersPage | `admin-app/src/context/AdminContext.tsx:2985-3019` — `useEffect` attaches `onSnapshot(collection(db, "members"), …)` directly, **missing the `auth.currentUser?.getIdToken(true)` wrapper** that MRB-15-09 added for reservations (line 1820-1832). `firestore.rules:229` `match /members/{userId} { allow read: if isStaff() \|\| (signedIn() && request.auth.uid == userId); … }` — rule reads `request.auth.token.role`. | **Real bug, MRB-15-09 pattern.** On long-idle sessions, fresh tabs, or right-after role-mint, the Firestore SDK's cached ID token is one refresh behind → listener attaches with stale token → every snapshot denied with `Missing or insufficient permissions` → `console.error` (line 3015) → `members` stays `[]`. `MembersPage.tsx:268-275` renders `<DataTable rows={filteredMembers} emptyMessage="No members match the current search." />` so the operator sees a faint empty-state card (looks like a blank screen). **Fix:** mirror reservations listener shape — wrap `onSnapshot` in `getIdToken(true).then(() => { if (cancelled) return; unsubscribe = onSnapshot(...) })` with `cancelled` race guard. Update existing `__tests__/admin-members-listener.test.ts:39-41` (which still pins the legacy `return unsubscribe` shape incompatible with the refresh pattern) to pin the new `.then → if (cancelled) → onSnapshot → return cleanup arrow` shape. ~30 lines + 1 new source-text test file. |

Submit handler at `:2628` writes `(possibly empty) reason` to Firestore: `updateBookingStatus(selectedBooking.id, "cancelled", { cancellationReason: reason }, { scope });`. **Same shape on the store-order cancel path** at `BookingsPage.tsx:6718-6730` and `:6753-6765` (both call `<ConfirmForm>` without `reasonRequired`). | **Real bug — separate from the checkout fix.** The subagent's first verbatim reading showed the label literally says "Cancellation reason (optional)" and the prop default is `false`. Pair with a date-arithmetic-free commit hash in DECISIONS-FEATURES.md once shipped. |
| **B-10 + B-10c** — Payment proof & Senior/PWD ID upload stuck on mobile, fail on desktop | GuestApp/BookingPage | `guest-app/src/pages/BookingPage.tsx:1330-1356` (`handlePaymentProofChange`) + `:1288-1328` (`handleDiscountChange` upload path). Handler shape: `validateUploadFile` (line 1008, 5 MB cap + MIME allowlist) → `compressImageFile` (line 1342 / 1313) → `uploadBytes(storageRef, …)` (line 1345 / 1313) → `setPaymentProofUpload / setDiscountIdUpload` → `try/catch` (lines 1349-1354) → `finally { setUploading…(false) }`. **No `AbortSignal` / no upload timeout.** | **Mostly environmental + one minor code fix.** The "stuck on Uploading" symptom is `uploadBytes()` never resolving on a hung Firebase Storage connection — the `finally` does run, but only on resolve/reject. **Recommended fix at `BookingPage.tsx:1345` (and the parallel ID path at `:1313`):** wrap `uploadBytes` in `Promise.race` with a 90 s timeout: `await Promise.race([uploadBytes(...), new Promise<never>((_, reject) => setTimeout(() => reject(new Error("Upload timed out after 90s. Please check your connection and retry.")), 90_000)])];`. This converts "stuck on Uploading forever" into the same error UI you already have at line 1351. Storage rules at `firebase/storage.rules:27-35` allow `write: if true` for both paths — no rule gate needed. Also worth checking on the env side: Firebase Storage bucket region match + CORS config; the 5 MB cap is client-side (line 92 `MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES`) so any file over 5 MB is rejected client-side, never reaching the server. |
| **#11** — Booking confirmation email never received | GuestApp/server | `guest-app/server/lib/resend.ts:3-9` — `RESEND_API_KEY` env var read; if missing falls back to `new Resend("re_mock_key")` with a `console.warn`. `guest-app/server/handlers/email.ts:898-905` — single `await resend.emails.send({ from: FROM_EMAIL, to, subject, html, … })` with **no try/catch around the Resend call** — throws uncaught on any Resend API error. The trigger at `guest-app/server/handlers/bookings.ts:9015-9032` (and the parallel trigger at `:4844-4848` and the legacy trigger at `:4856-4868`) wraps the `sendBookingTrigger` call in `try/catch` BUT the only catch action is `console.error("Failed to send booking confirmation email:", emailErr)` — **silent swallow**, no DLQ, no retry, no admin notification. The desk has no way to know the email failed. | **Real bug, partial environmental.** (1) **Code fix:** wrap the trigger's `try/catch` to (a) persist failed sends to a new `failed_emails` collection in Firestore (fields: `action`, `bookingId`, `error`, `lastAttemptAt`, `retryCount`); (b) surface an admin banner (use the existing NotificationCenter) on next admin dashboard load; (c) on the `booking-confirmed` path return `{ emailQueued: boolean }` to the staff UI so the desk can manually resend. (2) **Env check:** confirm `RESEND_API_KEY` is set in Vercel prod (not just staging) + `RESEND_FROM_EMAIL` is on a verified domain (Resend silently rejects unverified senders). New test file `booking-confirmed-email-persistence.test.ts` with 4 source-text guards (no try/catch around `resend.emails.send`, `console.error` swallow, lack of `failed_emails` collection write, lack of `{ emailQueued }` response shape). |
| **#22 + Q-03** — QR code print/download broken on mobile | AdminApp/QRManagement | `admin-app/src/pages/QRManagementPage.tsx:256-268` (`handleDownloadPng`) — canvas → `toDataURL("image/png")` → `<a download="…" href={pngUrl}>` → **`link.click()` on a DETACHED element** (no `document.body.appendChild(link)` first, no `removeChild` after). Chrome/Firefox honor detached `<a download>` clicks; **Safari iOS + macOS silently ignore the click** unless the element is in the DOM. Compare to the working pattern at `admin-app/src/pages/ReportsPage.tsx:3957-3962` which does `appendChild → click → removeChild`. Print path at `:233-240, 98-193` opens `window.open("")` (Safari iOS + most Android browsers block popups; the `onPopupBlocked` toast fires but the user gets nothing). | **Real DOM bug + browser-API gap, two fixes.** **(1) Download fix** — add `document.body.appendChild(link);` before `link.click();` and `document.body.removeChild(link);` after at `QRManagementPage.tsx:259-264` (mirror `ReportsPage.tsx:3957-3962`). This unblocks Safari desktop + iOS immediately; Android already works. **(2) Print fix** — replace the popup window with `window.print()` on the current page, plus a `@media print { … }` stylesheet that hides the room grid + shows the printable cards (no popup needed, works on every modern browser including Safari iOS). For iOS specifically, also surface a "Long-press the QR code above to save it as an image" hint when `navigator.userAgent` matches iPhone/iPad (the `<a download>` path is unreliable on iOS even with `appendChild`; the reliable iOS pattern is `navigator.share()` or direct user save via long-press on the in-page `<canvas>`). New test file `qr-management-mobile.test.ts` with 6 source-text guards (the appendChild presence, the removeChild presence, the `@media print` CSS rule, the `navigator.share` fallback, the iOS long-press hint, the print popup-not-required branch). |

(3) Add `data-testid="brand-wordmark"` on the wordmark span + `data-testid="notif-bell"` on the bell button + `data-testid="admin-header"` on the header root for e2e targeting.
